\mathcal{B}
sends

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1})

(124)

to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
and receives
\bot
or
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}v(\alpha)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}v(\beta)\oplus 1})
from the challenger.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})

and
\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{GateVrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
for each
i\in\{1,2,\cdots,j-1,j+1,j+2,\cdots,q\}
,
where
\mathsf{GateVrfy}
is described in Fig
4
.
If
\mathcal{B}
receives
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}v(\alpha)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}v(\beta)\oplus 1})
from the challenger,

\top\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
,

\top\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
,
and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GateVrfy}(\mathsf{cert}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i})
for all

i\in\{1,2,\cdots,j-1,j+1,j+2,\cdots,q\}
, then
\mathcal{B}
sends
\top
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

13.

\mathcal{B}
outputs the output of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j-1}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}=1]
.
Therefore, if for an adversary
\mathcal{A}
,

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j-1}=1]-\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}=1]}

is non-negligible, then

\allowbreak\left|\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)=1]-\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}\allowbreak(\lambda,1)=1]\right|

is non-negligible.
From
Lemma

B.3
, it contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

B.2
.

To show
Proposition

B.2
, it is sufficient to prove that the probability distribution of
\widetilde{C}
in
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
is statistically identical to
that of
\widetilde{C}
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
.

First, let us remind the difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
and
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
.
In both experiments ,
\widetilde{C}
consists of
\{\widetilde{g_{i}}\}_{i\in[q]}
and
\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]}
.
On the other hand the contents of
\{\widetilde{g_{i}}\}_{i\in[q]}
and
\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]}
are different in each experiments.
In
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
,

\widetilde{g_{i}}
consists of
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})

where

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}),

(125)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma_{b}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}\oplus\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}),

(126)

and

\widetilde{d_{i}}
is

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{0},0),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{1},1)].

(127)

In
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
,

\widetilde{g_{i}}
consists of
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})

where

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}),

(128)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{CT}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma_{b}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}\oplus\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}),

(129)

and

\widetilde{d_{i}}
is

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{0},C(x)_{i}),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{1},C(x)_{i}\oplus 1)].

(130)

The resulting distribution of
(\{\widetilde{g_{i}}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]})
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
is statistically identical to
the resulting distribution of
(\{\widetilde{g_{i}}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]})
in
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
.
This is because, at any level that is not output, the keys
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{1}
are used completely identically in the subsequent level so there is no difference
between always encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}
and
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}
.
At the output level, there is no difference between encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}
and giving the real mapping
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}\rightarrow v(c)

or encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}
and giving the programming mapping
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}\rightarrow C(x)_{i}
, which completes the proof.
∎

We use the following lemma for the proof of
Proposition

B.1
.
The proof is shown with the standard hybrid argument. It is also easy to see that a similar lemma holds for IND-CPA security.

Lemma B.3.

Let
\Sigma\coloneqq(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec},\mathsf{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a certified everlasting SKE scheme.
Let us consider the following security experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
against
\mathcal{A}
consisting of any QPT adversary
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
can call encryption queries. More formally, it can do the followings:
\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{SK}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to the challenger.

•

If
\beta=0
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and

(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
can repeat this process polynomially many times.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
generates
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
and chooses two triples of messages
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})\in\mathcal{M}^{3}
and
(x_{1},y_{1},z_{1})\in\mathcal{M}^{3}
, and sends
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1},\{x_{\sigma},y_{\sigma},z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger generates
(x,y,z)\leftarrow\mathcal{M}^{3}
.
The challenger computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{0})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{0},x),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},x\oplus x_{b})

(131)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{0})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},y),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{1},y\oplus y_{b})

(132)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{0})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},z),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},z\oplus z_{b})

(133)

and sends
\{\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
can call encryption queries. More formally, it can do the followings:

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{SK}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to the challenger.

•

If
\beta=0
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and

(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
can repeat this process polynomially many times.

6.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to the challenger, and sends the internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

7.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma})
,
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma})
and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{\sigma})
for each
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
. If all results are
\top
, then the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{sk}^{\prime\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

9.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

If the
\Sigma
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[ \expb{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1] - \Pr[ \expb{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 1)=1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

(134)

for any QPT adversary
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.3
.

We define the following hybrid experiment.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)
except that the challenger encrypts
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{1})
instead of encrypting
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except that the challenger encrypts
(x_{0},y_{1},z_{1})
instead of encrypting
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{1})
.

Lemma

B.3
easily follows from the following
Propositions

B.4
,
B.5
and
B.6
(whose proof is given later.).
∎

Proposition B.4.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Proposition B.5.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Proposition B.6.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]-\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Proof of
Proposition

B.4
.

We assume that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}(1)=1]}

is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that breaks the security experiment of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
.
This contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma
.
Let us describe how
\mathcal{B}
works.

1.

The challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
generates
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
, and
\mathcal{B}
generates
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{K}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to
\mathcal{B}
.

•

If
\beta=0
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, sends
m^{*}
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{m}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{m}^{0})
from the challenger, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

\mathcal{B}
repeats this process when
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
is sent from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathcal{B}
receives
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1},\{x_{\sigma},y_{\sigma},z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

4.

\mathcal{B}
generates
(x,y,z)\leftarrow\mathcal{M}^{3}
.

\mathcal{B}
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{0})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{0},x),(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},x\oplus x_{0}),

(135)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{1},y\oplus y_{0}),

(136)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},z\oplus z_{0}).

(137)

5.

\mathcal{B}
sets
m_{0}\coloneqq z
and
m_{1}\coloneqq z\oplus z_{0}\oplus z_{1}
.

\mathcal{B}
sends
(m_{0},m_{1})
to the challenger.

6.

The challenger computes
(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{0})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m_{b})
, and sends
\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{0}
to
\mathcal{B}
.

7.

\mathcal{B}
sends an encryption query
y
to the challenger, and receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{0})
.

8.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{CT}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{CT}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{CT}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

9.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{K}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to
\mathcal{B}
.

•

If
\beta=0
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, sends
m^{*}
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{m}^{0},\mathsf{CT}_{m}^{0})
from the challenger, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathsf{CT}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

\mathcal{B}
repeats this process when
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
is sent from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

10.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{x},\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{y},\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{z}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{B}
, and sends the internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

11.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{0}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0}
or
\bot
from the challenger.
If
\mathcal{B}
receives
\bot
, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

12.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{sk}^{\prime\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

13.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

14.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{x},\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma})
and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{y},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma})
for each
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
, and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{1})
.
If all results are
\top
,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)=1]
.
Since
z
is uniformly distributed,
(z,z\oplus z_{1})
and
(z\oplus z_{0}\oplus z_{1},z\oplus z_{0})
are identically distributed.
Therefore, it holds that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\cA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
.
∎

.

Appendix C
Proof of
Theorem

7.10

Proof of
Theorem

7.10
.

Let us describe how the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}
works.

\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathcal{V}=\{f(m),f,(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})\}\,\,or\,\,\emptyset
.

2.

If
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
, generate
f\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{s}
.

3.

Generate
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
and
L_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
.

4.

Compute
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
.

5.

Compute
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]},L_{i,f[i]})
and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

6.

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
and
\mathsf{CT}\coloneqq(\widetilde{U},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends

\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
If a key query is called,
the challenger receives an function
f
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, and sends
(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
,
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
, and

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},L_{i,\alpha})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
,
and sends
(\widetilde{U},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger,
and sends its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

6.

If
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert})
, and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

7.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
. If the challenger outputs
\top
, the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except for the following four points.
First, the challenger generates
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
if a key query is not called in step
2
.
Second, the challenger randomly generates
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]

and
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
in step
2
regardless of whether a key query is called or not.
Third, the challenger does not compute
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
in step
4
.
Fourth,
the challenger computes

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
instead of computing

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except for the following point.
The challenger computes
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},\allowbreak U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})

instead of computing
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

From the definition of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,b)
and
\mathsf{Sim}
,
it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Therefore,
Theorem

7.10
easily follows from the following
Propositions

C.1
and
C.2
. (whose proof is given later.)
∎

Proposition C.1.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(138)

Proposition C.2.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
satisfies the certified everlasting selective security,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(139)

Proof of
Proposition

C.1
.

For the proof, we use
Lemma

A.3
whose statement and proof is given in
Appendix

A
.
We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that breaks the security experiment of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
defined in
Lemma

A.3
.
This contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from
Lemma

A.3
.
Let us describe how
\mathcal{B}
works below.

1.

\mathcal{B}
receives
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
,
and sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
If a key query is called,

\mathcal{B}
receives an function
f
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, generates
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
and
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
.
If a key query is not called,
\mathcal{B}
generates
f\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{s}
,
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
and
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
.

3.

\mathcal{B}
sends
(f,L_{1,f[1]\oplus 1},L_{2,f[2]\oplus 1},\cdots,L_{s,f[s]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{1,f[1]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{2,f[2]\oplus 1},\cdots,L^{*}_{s,f[s]\oplus 1})
to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
.

4.

\mathcal{B}
receives
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]})
from the challenger.
If a key query is called,
\mathcal{B}
sends
(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to
\mathcal{B}
.

6.

\mathcal{B}
computes

(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
and

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]},\allowbreak L_{i,f[i]})
for every
i\in[s]
, and sends
(\widetilde{U},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

7.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathcal{B}
, and sends its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to the challenger, and receives
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
or
\bot
from the challenger. If
\mathcal{B}
receives
\bot
from the challenger, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

9.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

10.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

11.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Vrfy}
for
\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert}
and
\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}
for all
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]}
, and outputs
b^{\prime}
if all results are
\top
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]
and

\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\allowbreak\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]- \Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from
Lemma

A.3
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

C.2
.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that breaks the certified everlasting selective security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathcal{B}
works below.

1.

\mathcal{B}
generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends

\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
If a key query is called,
\mathcal{B}
receives an function
f
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, generates
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
, and sends
(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.
If a key query is not called,
\mathcal{B}
generates
f\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{s}
and
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to
\mathcal{B}
.

4.

\mathcal{B}
sends a circuit
U(\cdot,m)
and an input
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathcal{B},\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{selct}}(1^{\lambda},b)
.

5.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Samp}(1^{\lambda})
and does the following:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger computes
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
,
and sends
(\widetilde{U},\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathcal{B}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger computes
(\widetilde{U},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
,
and sends
(\widetilde{U},\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathcal{B}
.

6.

\mathcal{B}
computes
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]},L_{i,f[i]})
and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

7.

\mathcal{B}
sends
(\widetilde{U},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{CT}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

8.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger, and sends its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

9.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives
\top
or
\bot
from the challenger.
If
\mathcal{B}
receives
\bot
from the challenger, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

10.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

11.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

12.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}
for all
\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}
, and outputs
b^{\prime}
if all results are
\top
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible,
which contradicts the certified everlasting selective security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of
Theorem

7.12

Proof of
Theorem

7.12
.

For a given
2n
-qubit, let
A
be the
n
-qubit of the first half of the
2n
-qubit, and let
B
be the
n
-qubit of the second half of the
2n
-qubit.
Let
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Sim}
be the simulating algorithm of the ciphertext
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
.
Let us describe how the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}=(\mathsf{Sim}_{1},\mathsf{Sim}_{2},\mathsf{Sim}_{3})
works below.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))\,\,or\,\,\emptyset
.

5
5

5

If an adversary calls a key query before the adversary receives a challenge ciphertext,
then
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
.

2.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
does the following:

•

If
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
, generate
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and

\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.
Output
\mathsf{CT}\coloneqq(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}})
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

•

If
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))
, generate
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}),1^{|m|})
and
\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}
.
Output
\mathsf{CT}\coloneqq(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},a,c,1)
.

\mathsf{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{MSK},f,f(m),\mathsf{st})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
and
\mathsf{st}=(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

2.

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
.

3.

Compute
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}),1^{|m|})
.
Measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[N]
.

4.

Compute
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
.

5.

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{f}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}})
and
\mathsf{st}^{\prime}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x,z,1)
.

\mathsf{Sim}_{3}(\mathsf{st}^{*})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{st}^{*}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x^{*},z^{*},1)
or
\mathsf{st}^{*}=(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

2.

\mathsf{Sim}_{3}
does the following:

•

If the final bit of
\mathsf{st}^{*}
is
0
,
compute
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\emptyset,1^{|m|})
.
Measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[N]
.
Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x,z)
.

•

If the final bit of
\mathsf{st}^{*}
is
1
, output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x^{*},z^{*})
.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe},\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(0)
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to make an arbitrary key query at most one time.
For a key query, the challenger receives
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)

and
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,
computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
,

\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}

and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

5.

If a key query is not called in step
2
,

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to make an arbitrary key query at most one time.
For a key query, the challenger receives
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)

and
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

6.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to the challenger and its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

7.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Modify}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
.
The challenger computes
\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\allowbreak\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})
.
If the results are
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
and sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
. The output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
if the challenger outputs
\top
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
in the following second points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and sends
(\Psi,\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}

instead of computing
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))

and sending
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.
Second, in step
5
, the challenger computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(a,c))
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
instead of computing
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
and sending
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
in the following three points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger generates
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
instead of generating
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
and
\Psi=Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and
\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.
Second, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger sends
(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
instead of sending
(\Psi,\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and then that measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis for all
i\in[n]
.
Let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.
Third, the challenger computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
instead of computing
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(a,c))
in step
5
and computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Modify}(x,z,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
instead of computing

\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Modify}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
in step
7
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
in the following three points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger does not generate
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
and measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis in step
4
.
Second, if a key query is called in step
5
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)

and measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis for all
i\in[n]
after it computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
.
Third, if a key query is not called throughout the experiment,
the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis after step
5
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
except that the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
instead of computing
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, where
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f})
if a key query is called and
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
if a key query is not called.

From the definition of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,b)
and
\mathsf{Sim}=(\mathsf{Sim}_{1},\mathsf{Sim}_{2},\mathsf{Sim}_{3})
,
it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Therefore,
Theorem

7.12
easily follows from
Propositions

D.1
,
D.2
,
D.3
and
D.4
.
(Whose proof is given later.)
∎

Proposition D.1.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
is certified everlasting RNC secure,
it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(140)

Proposition D.2.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1].

(141)

Proposition D.3.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1].

(142)

Proposition D.4.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
is certified everlasting selective secure,
it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{3}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{4}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(143)

Proof of
Proposition

D.1
.

When an adversary makes key queries in step
2
, it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
Hence, we consider the case where the adversary does not make a key query in step
2
below.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that breaks the certified everlasting RNC security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathcal{B}
works below.

1.

\mathcal{B}
receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,b)
,
generates
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{B}
receives a message
m\in\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
, computes
\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}
, sends
(a,c)
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}^{*},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}^{*})
from the challenger, and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}^{*})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to send a key query at most one time.
For a key query,
\mathcal{B}
receives an function
f
, generates
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}^{*})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

4.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to
\mathcal{B}
and its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

5.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK}
or
\bot
from the challenger.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Modify}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})
.
If the result is
\top
and
\mathcal{B}
receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK}
from the challenger,

\mathcal{B}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
\bot
, sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

6.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

7.

\mathcal{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\allowbreak\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\allowbreak\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible,
which contradicts the certified everlasting RNC security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

D.2
.

We clarify the difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
.
First, in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
, the challenger uses
(x,z)
instead of using
(a,c)
as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
.
Second, in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
, the challenger sends
\Psi_{A}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
instead of sending
Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
.
Hence,
it is sufficient to prove that
x
and
z
are uniformly randomly distributed and

\Psi_{A}
is identical to
Z^{z}X^{x}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{x}Z^{z}
.
These two things are obvious from
Lemma

2.2
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

D.3
.

The difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
is only the order of operating the algorithm
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Enc}
and the Bell measurement on
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
.
Therefore, it is clear that the probability distribution of the ciphertext and the decryption key given to the adversary in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
is identical to that the ciphertext and the decryption key given to the adversary in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

D.4
.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{3}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{4}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that breaks the 1-bounded certified everlasting non-adaptive security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathcal{B}
works below.

1.

\mathcal{B}
receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,b)
,
generates
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
For a key query,
\mathcal{B}
receives
f
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, sends
f
to the challenger as a key query, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}
from the challenger, computes
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
and sends
m
to
\mathcal{B}
.

4.

\mathcal{B}
does the following.

•

If a key query is called in step
2
,
\mathcal{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
from the challenger, generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}X^{a}Z^{c}
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

•

If a key query is not called in step
2
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and
\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.

\mathcal{B}
computes
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and sends
(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{CT}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

5.

If a key query is not called in step
2
,

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to make a key query at most one time.
If
\mathcal{B}
receives an function
f
as key query,
\mathcal{B}
sends
f
to the challenger as key query, and receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}
from the challenger.

\mathcal{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
, measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis, and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.

6.

If
\mathcal{B}
does not receive a key query throughout the experiment,

\mathcal{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
, and measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{CT}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.

7.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to
\mathcal{B}
and its internal state to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Modify}(x^{*},z^{*},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
, where
(x^{*},z^{*})=(a,c)
if a key query is called in step
2
and
(x^{*},z^{*})=(x,z)
if a key query is not called in step
2
.

\mathcal{B}
sends
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK}
or
\bot
from the challenger.

\mathcal{B}
computes
\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
.
If the result is
\top
and
\mathcal{B}
receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK}
from the challenger,
\mathcal{B}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{B}
outputs
\bot
, sends
\bot
to
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

9.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

10.

\mathcal{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathcal{B}|b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{3}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{4}{}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the 1-bounded certified everlasting non-adaptive security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of
Theorem

7.14

Proof of
Theorem

7.14
.

Let us denote the simulating algorithm of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
as
\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Sim}=\mathsf{ONE}.(\mathsf{Sim}_{1},\mathsf{Sim}_{2},\mathsf{Sim}_{3})
.
Let us describe how the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}=(\mathsf{Sim}_{1},\mathsf{Sim}_{2},\mathsf{Sim}_{3})
works below.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|x|})
:

Let
q^{*}
be the number of times that
\mathcal{A}_{1}
has made key queries before it sends a challenge query.

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\{C_{j},C_{j}(x),(\Gamma_{j},\Delta_{j},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\}_{i\in[\Gamma_{j}]})\}_{j\in[q^{*}]}
.

2.

Generate a uniformly random set
\Gamma_{i}\subseteq[N]
of size
Dt+1
and a uniformly random set
\Delta_{i}\subseteq[S]
of size
v
for all
i\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\}
.
Let
\Delta_{0}\coloneqq\emptyset
. Let
\mathcal{L}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\neq i^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i^{\prime}})
.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
aborts if
\left|\mathcal{L}\right|>t
or
there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\setminus(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.

3.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
uniformly and independently samples
\ell
random degree
t
polynomials
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{\ell}
whose constant terms are all
0
.

4.

\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
samples the polynomials
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{S}
as follows for
j\in[q]
:

•

fix
a^{*}\in\Delta_{j}\setminus(\Delta_{0}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{j-1})
;

•

for all
a\in(\Delta_{j}\setminus(\Delta_{0}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{j-1}))\setminus\{a^{*}\}
, set
\xi_{a}
to be a uniformly random degree
Dt
polynomial whose constant term is
0
;

•

if
j\leq q^{*}
, pick a random degree
Dt
polynomial
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
whose constant term is
C_{j}(x)
; if
j>q^{*}
, pick random values for
\eta_{j}(i)
for all
i\in\mathcal{L}
;

•

the evaluation of
\xi_{a^{*}}
on the points in
\mathcal{L}
is defined by the relation:

\displaystyle\eta_{j}(\cdot)=C_{j}(\mu_{1}(\cdot),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(\cdot))+\sum_{a\in\Delta_{j}}\xi_{a}(\cdot).

(144)

•

Finally, for all
a\notin(\Delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{q})
, set
\xi_{a}
to be a uniformly random degree
Dt
polynomial whose constant term is
0
.

5.

For each
i\in\mathcal{L}
,
\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{CT}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},(\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i),\xi_{1}(i),\cdots,\xi_{S}(i))).

(145)

6.

For each
i\notin\mathcal{L}
,
\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
does the following:

•

If
i\in\Gamma_{j}
for some
j\in[q^{*}]

6
6

6

Note that
j
is uniquely determined since
i\notin\mathcal{L}
.

,
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{CT}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},(G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i},\eta_{j}(i),\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}),1^{|m|}).

(146)

•

If
i\notin\Gamma_{j}
for all
j\in[q^{*}]
,
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{CT}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},\emptyset,1^{|m|}).

(147)

7.

Output
\mathsf{CT}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{CT}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{j}(i)\}_{j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})
.

\mathsf{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{MSK},C_{j},C_{j}(x),\mathsf{st})
:

The simulator simulates the
j
-th key query for
j>q^{*}
.

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathsf{st}_{j-1}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{s}(i)\}_{s\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})
.

2.

For each
i\in\Gamma_{j}\cap\mathcal{L}
, generate
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j}})
.

3.

For each
i\in\Gamma_{j}\setminus\mathcal{L}
,
generate a random degree
Dt
polynomial
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
whose constant term is
C_{j}(x)
and subject to the constraints on the values in
\mathcal{L}
chosen earlier,
and generate

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}^{*})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},\eta_{j}(i),G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j}},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}).

(148)

For simplicity, let us denote
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}^{*}
as
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}
for
i\in\Gamma_{j}\setminus\mathcal{L}
.

4.

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j}}\coloneqq(\Gamma_{j},\Delta_{j},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{j}})
and

\mathsf{st}_{j}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{j}(i)\}_{j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})
.

\mathsf{Sim}_{3}(\mathsf{st}^{*})
:

The simulator simulates a verification key.

1.

Parse
\mathsf{st}^{*}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{j}(i)\}_{j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})
.

2.

For each
i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}
, compute
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Sim}_{3}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i})
.

3.

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
for
i\in[N]
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is allowed to call key queries at most
q
times.
For the
j
-th key query, the challenger receives an function

from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, generates a uniformly random set
\Gamma_{j}\in[N]
of size
Dt+1
and
\Delta_{j}\in[S]
of size
v
.
For

\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j}})
, and sends
(\Gamma_{j},\Delta_{j},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{j}})
to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.
Let
q^{*}
be the number of times that
\mathcal{A}_{1}
has called key queries in this step.

3.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
chooses

and sends

to the challenger.

t
polynomial
\mu_{i}(\cdot)

For

and sends

to

q-q^{*}
times.
For the
j
-th key query, the challenger receives an function
C_{j}
from
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, generates a uniformly random set
\Gamma_{j}\in[N]

.

outputs

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to

except for the following three points.

\Gamma_{i}\in[N]
of size

From the definition of

it is clear that

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Therefore,

easily follows from

,

,

and

∎

Proposition E.1.

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(149)

Proposition E.2.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1].

(150)

Proposition E.3.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
is 1-bounded certified everlasting adaptive secure,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{3}{}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(151)

Proposition E.4.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1].

(152)

Proof of
Proposition

E.1
.

Let
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}
be the experiment identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except that the challenger generates a set
\Gamma_{i}\in[N]
and
\Delta_{i}\in[S]
for
i\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\}

in step
4
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}=1]
.

Let
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}
be the experiment identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}
except that it outputs
\bot
if
|\mathcal{L}|>t
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}=1\wedge(|\mathcal{L}|\leq t)]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}=1\wedge(|\mathcal{L}|\leq t)]
.
Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{'}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{*}=1]}\leq\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]

(153)

from
Lemma

2.3
.

Let
\mathsf{Collide}
be the event that
there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\setminus(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}
is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
when
\mathsf{Collide}
does not occur.
Hence, it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}=1\wedge\overline{\mathsf{Collide}}]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1\wedge\overline{\mathsf{Collide}}]
.
Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{*}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}]

(154)

from
Lemma

2.3
.

From the discussion above, we have

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{0}{}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{1}{}=1]}\leq\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]+\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}].

(155)

The following
Lemmas

E.5
and
E.6
shows that

\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]\leq 2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

and
\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}]\leq q2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.5 (
[
GVW12
]
).

Let
\Gamma_{1},\cdots,\Gamma_{q}\subseteq[N]
be randomly chosen subsets of size
tD+1
.
Let
t=\Theta(q^{2}\lambda)
and
N=\Theta(D^{2}q^{2}t)
.
Then,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left|\bigcup_{i\neq i^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i})\right|>t\right]\leq 2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

(156)

where the probability is over the random choice of the subsets
\Gamma_{1},\cdots,\Gamma_{q}
.

Lemma E.6 (
[
GVW12
]
).

Let
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{q}\subseteq[S]
be randomly chosen subsets of size
v
.
Let
v(\lambda)=\Theta(\lambda)
and
S(\lambda)=\Theta(vq^{2})
.
Let
\mathsf{Collide}
be the event that there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\backslash(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Collide}\right]\leq q2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

(157)

where the probability is over the random choice of subsets
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{q}
.

Proof of
Proposition

E.2
.

In the encryption in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
,
\xi_{a^{*}}
is chosen at random and
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
is defined by the relation.

\mathsf{Sim}
essentially chooses
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
at random which defines
\xi_{a^{*}}
.
It is easy to see that reversing the order of how the polynomials are chosen produces the same distribution.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

E.3
.

To prove the proposition, let us define a hybrid experiment
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s}
for each
s\in[N]
as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
except for the following three points.
First, the challenger generates
\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{CT}_{i}\}_{i\in[s]\setminus\mathcal{L}}
as in the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}_{1}
.
Second, the challenger generates
\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{j}\cap[s]}
for
j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q^{\prime}\}
as in the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}_{2}
,
where
q^{\prime}
is the number of key queries that the adversary makes in total.
Third, the challenger generates
\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[s]\setminus\mathcal{L}}
as in the simulator
\mathsf{Sim}_{3}
.

Let us denote
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{0}
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{N}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]
.
Furthermore, we can show that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s-1}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

(158)

for
s\in[N]
. (Its proof is given later.)
From these facts, we obtain

E.3
.

Let us show the remaining one.

, it is clear that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s-1}

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s}
.

.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s-1}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s}=1]}

Then, we can construct an adversary
\mathcal{B}
that can break the 1-bounded certified everlasting adaptive security of

as follows.

sets

.

for all

, and sends

times.

from

and

of size
v
.
For
i\in\Gamma_{j}\setminus s
,
\mathcal{B}
generates
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j}})
.
If
s\in\Gamma_{j}
,

to the challenger,

to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
.
Let

be the number of times that

has called key queries in this step.

chooses
x\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends

to
\mathcal{B}
.

\mathcal{B}
generates uniformly random set

of size

and

of size

.

\mathcal{B}

for

.

.

is allowed to call key queries at most

times.

-th key query,

.

It is clear that

and

.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s-1}=1]-\Pr[\sfhyb{2}{s}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\cB|b=0]-\Pr[1\la\cB|b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the 1-bounded certified everlasting adaptive security of

.

Proof of
Proposition

E.4
.

In
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
, the polynomials
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{\ell}
are chosen with constant terms
x_{1},\cdots,x_{\ell}
, respectively.
In
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
, these polynomials are now chosen with
0
constant terms.
This only affects the distribution of
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{\ell}
themselves and polynomials
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{S}
.
Moreover, only the evaluations of these polynomials on the points in
\mathcal{L}
affect the outputs of the experiments.
Now observe that:

•

The distribution of the values
\{\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i)\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}}
are identical to both
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
.
This is because in both experiments, we choose these polynomials to be random degree
t
polynomials (with different constraints in the constant term),
so their evaluation on the points in
\mathcal{L}
are identically distributed, since
|\mathcal{L}|\leq t
.

•

The values
\{\xi_{1}(i),\cdots,\xi_{S}(i)\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}}
depend only on the values
\{\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i)\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}}
.

Proposition

E.4
follows from these observations.
∎
